Method: A dual-branch contrastive learning framework with (1) a spatial branch using LMM-generated textual descriptions from street-view images as semantic priors, and (2) a temporal branch using a month encoder for monthly emission variation. Multimodal data is used only during pretraining; inference requires only satellite imagery.

Result: CarbonCLIP outperforms baselines in both Beijing and Singapore, demonstrating effective transfer of multimodal knowledge into satellite representations for carbon modeling.

Conclusion: The proposed method bridges satellite views and ground-level activities via task-oriented multimodal distillation, offering a robust and scalable solution for satellite-based urban carbon modeling.

Abstract: Accurately estimating urban carbon emissions is critical for sustainable urban planning, yet many existing approaches remain difficult to apply consistently across cities due to data-source heterogeneity and the lack of fine-grained semantic-temporal context in remote sensing data. We propose CarbonCLIP, a task-oriented multimodal distillation framework that improves satellite-based carbon emission prediction by transferring contextual knowledge into a unified satellite representation through dual-branch contrastive learning. Unlike conventional methods that rely on static visual features, CarbonCLIP explicitly bridges the gap between top-down satellite views and ground-level human activities. Specifically, the spatial branch uses fine-grained textual descriptions automatically generated from street-view images by Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) to provide semantic priors reflecting building functions, infrastructure, and urban activities, while the temporal branch employs a month encoder to encode temporal priors associated with monthly emission variation. CarbonCLIP requires multimodal data only during the pretraining phase; during inference, it relies solely on satellite imagery, thereby supporting scalable deployment when ground-level data are unavailable at inference. Experiments on Beijing and Singapore demonstrate that CarbonCLIP outperforms baselines in both study cities. The results validate that our method effectively transfers multimodal knowledge into satellite representations, offering a robust solution for satellite-based urban carbon modeling.

</details>


### [51] [SoccerNet 2026 Challenges Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07320)
*Anthony Cioppa,Silvio Giancola,Håkan Ardö,Mohamad Dalal,Jan Held,Jérémie Ochin,Jiayuan Rao,Karen Sanchez,Renaud Vandeghen,Artur Xarles,Olivier Barnich,Albert Clapés,Mathieu Delvaux,Sergio Escalera,Bernard Ghanem,Cédric Hons,Antoine Houet,Sotiris Manitsaris,Tom Michel,Pierre Miralles,Thomas B. Moeslund,Mikael Nilsson,Bogdan Stanciulescu,Marc Van Droogenbroeck,Yanfeng Wang,Weidi Xie,Faisal Altawijri,Mohamed Atef,Semen Budennyy,Vasiliy Chelpanov,Puhua Chen,Yixin Chen,Lechao Cheng,Jianling Chu,Ju-Seong Do,Oleg Durygin,Omar Fetouh,Mirco Fuchs,Youssef Ghallab,Falguni Ghosh,Wonjun Heo,Yufeng Hu,Weixuan Huang,Phuong-Linh Huynh-Ha,Matvey Isupov,Yangguang Ji,Siyuan Jiang,Zhenxiang Jiang,Wonyong Jo,Ho-Young Jung,SeongHeon Kang,MinJae Kim,Youngseon Kim,Jakub Komosa,Artem Konshin,Trung-Hoang Le,Jongmin Lee,Lingling Li,Litao Li,Vadim Linkov,Fang Liu,Haoxuan Ma,Shun Makino,Ismail Mathkour,Konstantin Mitin,Mikhail Moiseev,Takumi Nagaya,Yuki Nakamura,Thanh-Khoi Nguyen,Hoang-Phuc Nguyen,Trong-Thuan Nguyen,Christian Orduz,Kwanyong Park,Fabian Perez,Parthsarthi Rawat,SuHyun Rim,Hoover Rueda-Chacón,Atom Scott,Minori Sugimura,Yuyang Sun,Shengeng Tang,Minh-Triet Tran,Ikuma Uchida,Juan Vanegas,Thanh-Nhan Vo,Jiangtao Wang,Yaxiong Wang,Xiaogang Wang,Ruifeng Wang,Rio Watanabe,Jiali Wen,Yongliang Wu,Di Yang,Xu Yang,Zhuo Yang,Xinyu Ye,Yibo Yu,Zihan Zhai,Yu Zhang,Zhenyu Zhao,Zhun Zhong,Yixi Zhou,Xingyu Zhu,Wenbo Zhu,Julian Ziegler*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The SoccerNet 2026 Challenges present five computer vision tasks for sports video understanding (Ball Action Anticipation, Player-Centric Ball Action Spotting, Novel View Synthesis, Spiideo SoccerNet Synloc, and Visual Question Answering), with 427 teams submitting 1,129 entries, and this paper documents the tasks, evaluation protocols, leaderboards, and leading submissions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance computer vision research in sports video understanding through an annual open benchmarking effort, providing a unified platform for evaluating state-of-the-art methods on challenging, real-world football video analysis tasks.

Method: The challenge organized five vision-based tasks with annotated data, unified evaluation protocols, and public baselines. Participants submitted entries and technical reports, which were reviewed and compiled into leaderboards across tasks.

Result: 427 teams submitted 1,129 entries across five tasks; 28 teams contributed reviewed technical reports. Leaderboards and leading submissions are presented for each task.

Conclusion: The paper documents the current state of each computer vision task as measured on held-out challenge data, providing a benchmark reference for future research in sports video understanding.

Abstract: The SoccerNet 2026 Challenges constitute the sixth annual edition of the SoccerNet open benchmarking effort, dedicated to advancing computer vision research in sports video understanding. This year's challenges span five vision-based tasks: (1) Ball Action Anticipation, predicting the timing and class of ball-related actions within a short future window from a preceding observation window; (2) Player-Centric Ball Action Spotting, temporally localizing and classifying ball-related actions while assigning each action to the acting player through team affiliation and jersey number; (3) Novel View Synthesis, rendering images from unobserved camera poses in multi-view football scenes; (4) Spiideo SoccerNet Synloc, localizing athletes in real-world pitch coordinates from a single calibrated static-camera image; and (5) Visual Question Answering, answering multiple-choice questions about football broadcasts across text, image, and video inputs. For each task, participants were provided with annotated data, a unified evaluation protocol, and a public baseline. This edition saw broad participation, with 427 teams submitting 1,129 entries across the five tasks and 28 teams contributing reviewed technical reports. This paper describes each task and its evaluation protocol, presents the challenge leaderboards, and summarizes the leading submissions, with the aim of documenting the current state of each task as measured on held-out challenge data.

</details>


### [52] [HAJJv2-CrowdCount: Zero-Shot Benchmark for Dense Crowd Counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07322)
*Reem AlYabis,Fares AlTuwaim,AlJawharh AlOtaibi,Mohamed Eltahir*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces HAJJv2-CrowdCount, a per-second annotated crowd counting dataset for Hajj videos, and benchmarks three zero-shot counting paradigms (YOLO-World, APGCC, SAM3Count). While SAM3Count achieves the lowest overall MAE (70.4), the point-based counter APGCC performs best on dense, heavily occluded frames (MAE 114.9 vs. >300 for others), a critical finding for real Hajj crowd management deployment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Automated crowd counting in Hajj videos is difficult because existing models fail due to steep camera angles, extensive occlusion, and extremely high crowd densities. Existing benchmarks for this environment are either private or lack per-second granularity, motivating the creation of detailed annotations and a systematic benchmark.

Method: The authors annotate the HAJJv2 testing videos with per-second human-annotated crowd counts (HAJJv2-CrowdCount). They benchmark three zero-shot paradigms: an open-vocabulary detector (YOLO-World), a point-based counter (APGCC), and a promptable segmentation-based counter (SAM3Count), evaluating MAE overall and separately on the densest frames.

Result: SAM3Count achieves the best overall MAE (70.4, 95% CI 56.0-86.1), followed by YOLO-World (92.0) and APGCC (152.9). However, on the densest frames, SAM3Count and YOLO-World degrade sharply (MAE >300), while APGCC degrades far more gracefully (MAE 114.9).

Conclusion: The performance inversion on dense frames is decision-relevant for Hajj crowd management: point-based counting (APGCC) is more reliable where counts are needed most. The dataset is released for reproducibility and extension.

Abstract: Automated crowd counting in Hajj video is difficult not because current models lack capacity, but because the footage violates the assumptions those models were built on: cameras observe the crowd from steep, near-vertical angles, individuals occlude one another extensively, and a single frame can contain well over a thousand people. Benchmarks that test crowd counting in such an environment are either private or not detailed per second. We revisit the HAJJv2 dataset and contribute HAJJv2-CrowdCount: per-second human-annotated crowd counts for its testing videos. Using these annotations, we benchmark three recent zero-shot counting paradigms: an open-vocabulary detector (YOLO-World), a point-based counter (APGCC), and a promptable segmentation-based counter (SAM3Count). SAM3Count attains the lowest overall mean absolute error (MAE 70.4, 95% CI 56.0-86.1), ahead of YOLO-World (92.0) and APGCC (152.9). This ordering reverses, however, in the regime most relevant to deployment: on the densest frames, the detection- and segmentation-based counters both degrade sharply (MAE exceeding 300), while the point-based counter degrades far more gracefully (MAE 114.9). This inversion is decision-relevant for Hajj crowd management, where reliable counts are needed most precisely in the densest and most occluded scenes. The annotations are released to support reproduction and extension of these results.

</details>


### [53] [BUS: Brain-Inspired Unsupervised Self-Reflection for Advanced Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07361)
*Jiacheng Yang,Tongying Xiao,Yunkai Dang,Cong Wang,Yuekun Yang,Qi Fan,Wenbin Li,Feng Miao,Yang Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes BUS (Brain-inspired Unsupervised Self-reflection), a label-free training framework that enhances VLM reasoning by leveraging backward prediction (predicting preceding states from future states), inspired by neuroscience. It eliminates the need for annotated data, works with SFT and RL fine-tuning, and shows strong results across 8 benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current VLMs struggle with complex visual tasks requiring consistent and fine-grained reasoning. Existing self-reflective reasoning methods require large annotated datasets and lack explicit reflective behavior during test time. The authors aim to bridge this gap by drawing inspiration from the brain's backward prediction mechanism.

Method: The authors first verify that mainstream VLMs can perform backward prediction similar to the human brain. They then propose BUS, an unsupervised self-reflection framework that: (1) uses backward prediction to generate explicit learning signals, (2) operates on unlabeled data, and (3) is compatible with SFT and RL fine-tuning methods.

Result: Experiments on 8 benchmarks demonstrate that BUS achieves notable improvements over base models across a wide range of complex visual tasks, using only unlabeled training data.

Conclusion: Backward prediction capability is critical for VLM reasoning. The proposed BUS framework effectively enhances reflective reasoning without requiring annotated data, and is compatible with popular fine-tuning approaches.

Abstract: Current Vision-Language Models (VLMs) often struggle to handle complex visual tasks that require consistent and fine-grained reasoning. Recent methods aim to train models to facilitate self-reflective reasoning, i.e., reviewing and improving the generated reasoning. However, they require large volumes of annotated data and lack explicit reflective behavior during test time. This work aims to bridge this gap through inspiration from neuroscience. The human brain exhibits efficient backward prediction, i.e., predicting which current states are likely to precede a given future state. In this work, we first verify that mainstream VLMs can perform backward prediction, similar to the human brain. Then, we propose Brain-inspired Unsupervised Self-reflection (BUS), a label-free training framework to enhance reflective reasoning capability in challenging image analysis. BUS enables VLMs to perform backward prediction and provide explicit learning signals on data without ground-truth labels. In this way, BUS eliminates reliance on annotated data while improving reasoning performance. Notably, BUS is compatible with popular fine-tuning methods, such as Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) and Reinforcement Learning (RL). Finally, extensive experiments on 8 benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of BUS across a wide range of complex visual tasks. It achieves notable improvements over the base model while using only unlabeled training data. Our experimental findings validate that backward prediction capability is critical for VLM reasoning.

</details>


### [54] [MMAgent-R$^2$: Learning to Rerank and Reject for Agentic mRAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07383)
*Tao Zhang,Ziqi Zhang,Zongyang Ma,Yuxin Yang,Bing Li,Chunfeng Yuan,Kang Rong,Fengyun Rao,Jing Lyu,Weiming Hu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MMAgent-R² is an agentic mRAG framework for KB-VQA that integrates visual reranking and active rejection as internal verification mechanisms, achieving SOTA on InfoSeek, E-VQA, and MMhops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing mRAG methods rely on global visual features for entity retrieval, which fails to distinguish visually similar but factually mismatched entities in large-scale KBs. Since subsequent noise filtering is confined to this fixed candidate set, retrieval errors propagate to the final answer.

Method: Proposes MMAgent-R² with: (1) visual reranking that directly compares query and candidate images for discriminative matching; (2) active rejection that discards unreliable results and retrieves additional candidates; (3) a composite reward function with step-level verification rewards; (4) joint optimization of retrieval, verification, and generation via GRPO training.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on InfoSeek, E-VQA, and MMhops, with notable advantages in challenging retrieval scenarios and complex multi-image multi-hop reasoning tasks.

Conclusion: The agentic framework with internal verification (reranking + active rejection) effectively mitigates retrieval error propagation and improves KB-VQA performance, especially under visually ambiguous and multi-hop reasoning conditions.

Abstract: Knowledge-based Visual Question Answering (KB-VQA) requires models to retrieve visual entities matching the query image from large-scale encyclopedic knowledge bases and answer related questions. Existing multimodal Retrieval Augmented Generation (mRAG) methods rely on global visual features to match candidate entities, yet when the knowledge base contains numerous visually similar entities, the retriever struggles to distinguish them, populating the candidate set with visually similar but factually mismatched distractors. Since subsequent processing steps such as noise filtering are also confined to this fixed candidate set, errors from failed retrieval inevitably propagate to the final answer. To address these challenges, we propose MMAgent-R$^2$, an agentic mRAG framework that integrates visual reranking and active rejection as its internal verification mechanism. Visual reranking directly compares query and candidate images, capturing discriminative details beyond textual descriptions to precisely identify the target entity among similar candidates; active rejection discards unreliable results and retrieves additional candidates when no confident match is found, moving beyond the fixed candidate pool. We design a composite reward function with step-level verification rewards and achieve joint optimization of external retrieval, internal verification, and answer generation via GRPO training. Experiments on InfoSeek, E-VQA, and MMhops demonstrate that \ours{} achieves state-of-the-art performance, with particularly notable advantages in challenging retrieval scenarios and complex multi-image multi-hop reasoning tasks.

</details>


### [55] [When Prompts Ignore Structure: Graph-Based Attribute Reasoning for Calibrated VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07395)
*Tanay Sodha,Aditya Sharma,Ramya Hebbalaguppe,Vinti Agarwal,Pranav Murthy Yeluripaty*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ARGTCA introduces a graph-based approach using Symbolic Attribute Graphs and Graph Attention Networks with contrastive learning to improve calibration in test-time adaptation of VLMs, reducing ECE by ~37% with a diversity-based attribute selection strategy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Test-time adaptation in VLMs suffers from poor calibration (entropy-driven overconfidence) because existing methods treat class attributes independently and ignore their relational structure, leading to degraded confidence estimation.

Method: ARGTCA models (class, attribute) pairs as nodes in a Symbolic Attribute Graph, trains a Graph Attention Network (GAT) using contrastive objectives to capture inter-attribute dependencies, and introduces two attribute selection strategies: ARGTCA-DIV (intra-class diversity) and ARGTCA-DISC (inter-class discrimination).

Result: Across nine benchmarks, ARGTCA-DIV reduces average ECE by ~37% over baselines; ARGTCA-DISC reduces average ECE by ~17%, consistently ranking as the second-best variant.

Conclusion: Modeling symbolic attribute interactions via graph-structured representations is a principled and effective approach for achieving reliable (well-calibrated) test-time adaptation in VLMs.

Abstract: Reliable confidence estimation remains a key limitation of test-time adaptation in vision-language models (VLMs), where prompt tuning improves zero-shot accuracy but often degrades calibration due to entropy-driven overconfidence. Prior approaches mitigate this using LLM-derived class attributes and contrastive regularization, yet treat attributes independently, ignoring their relational structure. We propose ARGTCA, which represents (class, attribute) pairs as nodes in a Symbolic Attribute Graph and trains a Graph Attention Network (GAT) using contrastive objectives to produce structurally informed embeddings that capture inter-attribute dependencies. We introduce two attribute selection strategies: ARGTCA-DIV for intra-class diversity and ARGTCA-DISC for inter-class discrimination. Experiments across nine benchmarks show that ARGTCA-DIV reduces average Expected Calibration Error (ECE) by approximately ~37% over baselines, while ARGTCA-DISC consistently performs as the second-best variant, reducing average ECE by approximately ~17% over baselines. These results suggest that modeling symbolic attribute interactions provides a principled approach for reliable test-time adaptation in VLMs.

</details>


### [56] [Heterogeneity-Adaptive Diffusion Schrodinger Bridge for PET-Guided Whole-Body MRI Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07401)
*Chengbo Wang,Jiacheng Yu,Linjie Bian,Ming Qi,Xiaosheng Liu,Tongtong Che,Jichang Zhang,Shuyu Li,Shaoli Song,Xiuying Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HA-DSB is a deep learning framework for whole-body PET-MRI translation that uses a Heterogeneity-Adaptive Diffusion Schrödinger Bridge with VLM-based region context embeddings and PET-guided dual-stage noise modulation to handle heterogeneous anatomical regions and pathological tissues, reducing acquisition time while improving translation quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Whole-body PET-MR scanning suffers from excessively long acquisition times, hindering clinical efficiency. Existing deep learning MRI translation models focus on specific anatomical regions and struggle with whole-body scans due to highly heterogeneous feature distributions across different anatomical regions and pathological/lesion tissues.

Method: HA-DSB uses a Diffusion Schrödinger Bridge for stochastic transport between source and target distributions. It incorporates (1) region context embeddings from a vision-language model (VLM) for region-specific modeling, (2) a PET-guided noise modulation module that adaptively scales spatial diffusion perturbations during the forward process, and (3) an attention mechanism leveraging PET features during the reverse process to amplify lesion-relevant structures.

Result: The method demonstrates superiority across different body regions in whole-body MRI translation and shows improved translation quality in lesion areas under PET guidance.

Conclusion: The HA-DSB framework effectively addresses whole-body MRI translation challenges by handling heterogeneity across anatomical regions and pathological tissues, offering a potential solution to reduce PET-MR scan duration.

Abstract: While whole-body multimodal medical imaging scanners have been increasingly recognized for more effective medical applications, the excessive long acquisition time in PET-MR scanning is a major obstacle in more efficient clinical practice. Deep learning-based MRI translation provides a potential solution to reduce scan duration. However, current models often focus on specific anatomical regions and face challenges for whole-body scans that consists of highly heterogeneous feature distributions mainly due to (1) different anatomical regions across whole-body, and (2) lesions or pathological tissues. This paper tackles the challenges through a novel Heterogeneity-Adaptive Diffusion Schrodinger Bridge (HA-DSB) framework. By explicitly modeling translation as stochastic transport between source and target distributions, HA-DSB incorporates region context embeddings derived from a vision-language model (VLM) to enable region-specific modeling. To enhance fidelity of the pathological tissue, lesion-aware metabolic prior from PET is integrated directly into the bridge dynamics through a dual-stage guidance mechanism. Specifically, a PET-guided noise modulation module adaptively scales spatial diffusion perturbations during the forward process, while PET features are leveraged during the reverse process to selectively amplify lesion-relevant structures via an attention mechanism. Experiments demonstrate the superiority of our method across different body regions in whole-body MRI translation and show improved translation quality in lesion areas under PET guidance. Our code is available at Github.

</details>


### [57] [VCDP: Variation-Conditioned Distributional Proxy Learning for Semi-Supervised Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07416)
*Zimu Zhang,Yiheng Zhong,Zhuoru Zhang,Yingzhen Hu,Yanan He,Fanliang Meng,Xiaofeng Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VCDP is a plug-and-play training-only regularization module for semi-supervised 3D medical image segmentation that uses class-wise learnable Gaussian distributions and variation prototypes to improve feature-space organization for anatomically complex structures, enhancing performance on small and ambiguous organs without additional inference cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing semi-supervised 3D medical image segmentation methods (consistency regularization, pseudo-labeling, co-training) improve prediction-level robustness but fail to provide sufficient feature-space organization for anatomically complex structures, particularly small organs and ambiguous boundary regions with large intra-class variations.

Method: VCDP represents each class with (1) a learnable Gaussian distribution for shared class semantics and (2) multiple variation prototypes for fine-grained intra-class patterns. A variation-conditioned compatibility score fuses distributional similarity and soft variation aggregation to guide voxel embeddings toward both global organ identity and local anatomical variations. It is attached to decoder features during training and removed during inference.

Result: Experiments on multi-organ segmentation benchmarks demonstrate that VCDP improves most evaluated baselines, especially for small, ambiguous, and highly variable organs.

Conclusion: VCDP effectively addresses feature-space disorganization in semi-supervised 3D medical image segmentation by modeling both global class semantics and local intra-class variations, achieving performance gains without inference overhead.

Abstract: Semi-supervised 3D medical image segmentation reduces the need for dense voxel-level annotations by exploiting unlabeled volumes. Although existing methods such as consistency regularization, pseudo-labeling, and co-training improve prediction-level robustness, they often provide insufficient feature-space organization for anatomically complex structures, especially small organs and ambiguous boundary regions with large intra-class variations. To address this issue, we propose Variation-Conditioned Distributional Proxy Learning (VCDP), a plug-and-play training-only regularization module for semi-supervised 3D medical image segmentation. VCDP represents each class with a learnable Gaussian distribution for shared class semantics and multiple variation prototypes for fine-grained intra-class patterns. A unified variation-conditioned compatibility score is further formulated to fuse distributional similarity and soft variation aggregation, guiding voxel embeddings to align with both global organ identity and local anatomical variations. VCDP is attached to decoder features during training and removed during inference, introducing no additional inference cost. Experiments on multi-organ segmentation benchmarks show that VCDP improves most evaluated baselines, particularly for small, ambiguous, and highly variable organs. Our anonymous code is released at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/VCDP_code-41ED.

</details>


### [58] [Two-Stage Multi-Modal Fusion with Adaptive Alignment for Action Quality Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07438)
*Kanglei Zhou,Ruizhi Cai,Xinning Wang,Yijian Zheng,Liyuan Wang,Jianguo Li,Xiaohui Liang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DualAlign is a two-stage multi-modal fusion framework with adaptive alignment for Action Quality Assessment, which first aligns visual modalities (RGB, optical flow, skeleton) and then incorporates text, outperforming SOTA on MM-JDM (+21.16%), RG (+3.53%), and Fis-V (+5.95%) benchmarks while being robust to missing modalities and label scarcity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unimodal AQA methods struggle to capture subtle movement quality cues; multi-modal methods face cross-modal misalignment, unstable fusion, and costly annotations leading to limited dataset diversity.

Method: DualAlign: a two-stage multi-modal fusion framework. Stage 1: construct coherent visual representation by maximizing shared structural info across RGB, optical flow, and skeleton. Stage 2: incorporate textual semantics after visual stabilization to complement without distorting visual manifold. Also introduces MM-JDM dataset with RGB, flow, skeleton, and structured text exhibiting modality noise, class imbalance, and label scarcity.

Result: DualAlign improves average correlation on MM-JDM by 21.16% over SOTA, achieves gains of 3.53% on RG and 5.95% on Fis-V benchmarks, and remains robust under missing-modality and label-scarce conditions.

Conclusion: The two-stage adaptive alignment strategy effectively addresses cross-modal misalignment and fusion instability, and the MM-JDM benchmark provides a realistic challenging testbed for multi-modal AQA research.

Abstract: Action Quality Assessment (AQA) aims to evaluate how well a person performs a movement, which is essential in applications such as sports scoring, skill assessment, and healthcare. However, unimodal approaches often struggle to capture subtle cues of movement quality in real-world settings. Although multi-modal inputs provide complementary information, existing methods still face two major challenges: heterogeneous modalities often lead to cross-modal misalignment and unstable fusion, and reliable multi-modal annotation is costly, resulting in limited dataset diversity. To address these challenges, we propose DualAlign, a two-stage multi-modal fusion framework with adaptive alignment. The framework first constructs a coherent visual representation by maximizing shared structural information across RGB video, optical flow, and skeleton modalities. Textual semantics are then incorporated after visual stabilization, allowing high-level descriptions to complement rather than distort the underlying visual manifold. To evaluate the framework under realistic multi-modal conditions, we introduce MM--JDM, a movement-quality assessment dataset integrating RGB videos, optical flow, skeleton sequences, and structured text. MM--JDM naturally exhibits modality noise, class imbalance, and label scarcity, making it a challenging benchmark for studying multi-modal fusion and alignment. Extensive experiments show that DualAlign improves average correlation on MM--JDM by 21.16% over the state-of-the-art methods and achieves gains of 3.53% and 5.95% on the RG and Fis-V benchmarks, respectively. DualAlign also remains robust under missing-modality and label-scarce conditions.

</details>


### [59] [A Theory of Contrastive Learning with Natural Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07470)
*Antonio Torralba,Yair Weiss*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper analytically derives the optimal representations learned by contrastive learning on images with stationary statistics, showing that the first-layer filters become sinusoids followed by pointwise nonlinearity, global average pooling, and partial whitening. A waterfilling algorithm computes the frequencies and weights from the dataset's power spectrum, and experiments confirm SGD-trained CNNs learn these structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand why contrastive learning on simple images and augmentations produces useful representations for downstream tasks by analytically deriving the optimal representation.

Method: Analytically computed optimal representation for contrastive loss using basic augmentations on image datasets with stationary statistics; derived that optimal CNN first-layer filters are sinusoids followed by pointwise nonlinearity, global average pooling, and partial whitening; used a waterfilling algorithm based on the dataset's expected power spectrum.

Result: For certain augmentations, the optimal CNN first-layer filters are sinusoids; for more complex augmentations, optimal weights remain sinusoids. The frequencies and weights are computable via a waterfilling algorithm using the dataset's power spectrum. Empirical experiments with SGD-trained CNNs confirm they learn sinusoids in the first layer and perform partial whitening.

Conclusion: Contrastive learning's effectiveness can be theoretically explained: it learns sinusoidal filters and partial whitening, with the structure determined by the dataset's power spectrum via a simple waterfilling algorithm.

Abstract: Why does contrastive learning with simple images and augmentations yield useful representations for downstream tasks? We address this question by analytically computing the optimal representation in terms of a contrastive loss for a range of basic augmentations and any image dataset with stationary statistics. We show that for certain augmentations the optimum can be attained by a CNN whose first layer filters are sinusoids, followed by a pointwise nonlinearity, global average pooling, and a final linear layer that performs partial whitening. We also show that the optimal weights in such CNNs for more complicated augmentations are still sinusoids. The frequencies of the sinusoids and their weights can be computed using a simple waterfilling algorithm given the dataset's expected power spectrum. Experiments with different image datasets and augmentations show that such CNNs trained with SGD empirically learn sinusoids in their first layer and to perform partial whitening

</details>


### [60] [Discovering Geometric Biases in 3D Face Reconstruction: A Curvature-Aware Spectral Framework for Fairness Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07486)
*Veronika Shilova,Emmanuel Malherbe,Giovanni Palma,Panagiotis-Alexandros Bokaris,Laurent Risser,Jean-Michel Loubes*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a curvature-aware evaluation framework using the Laplace-Beltrami Operator to detect, quantify, and visualize demographic biases (age, gender, ethnicity) in 3DMM-based face reconstruction, showing that curvature-based metrics correlate better with human perception than traditional Euclidean distance measures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard 3DMMs inherit morphological biases from limited training data, reducing generalizability across diverse populations. Existing evaluation metrics (Euclidean distances) fail to capture subtle surface nuances and demographic biases, motivating the need for a more geometrically meaningful and fairness-aware evaluation protocol.

Method: The framework uses the Laplace-Beltrami Operator (LBO) to compute high-resolution curvature error maps between ground truth and reconstructed meshes. A novel curvature-derived error metric is proposed and validated via a user study for correlation with human perception. Experiments are conducted across multiple 3DMM bases and fitting algorithms to analyze biases.

Result: The curvature-based metric shows significantly higher correlation with human perception compared to traditional Euclidean-based methods. Systematic age-related biases and preliminary evidence of gender- and ethnicity-related biases are uncovered across different 3DMM bases and fitting algorithms.

Conclusion: Curvature-aware evaluation protocols are necessary to ensure demographic fairness and geometric precision in 3D face reconstruction. The findings advocate for adopting such metrics to mitigate bias and improve generalizability of 3DMM-based methods.

Abstract: 3D Morphable Models (3DMMs) remain the standard parametric shape priors for many state-of-the-art 3D face reconstruction algorithms. However, as these models are derived from a finite number of 3D face samples, they inherit the morphological biases of their training data, potentially limiting their generalizability across diverse global populations. In this paper, we propose a novel framework to analyze 3DMM reconstructions through the lens of surface curvature, with the objective to discover, quantify and visualize biases. While standard evaluation metrics often rely on Euclidean distances, our reconstruction error captures subtle surface nuances such as local topology or undulations. To do so, we leverage the Laplace-Beltrami Operator (LBO) to generate high-resolution curvature error maps, providing a localized and geometrically meaningful visualization of discrepancies between ground truth faces and reconstructed meshes. We derive from it an error metric that we validated through a user study, observing a significantly higher correlation to human perception compared to traditional methods. Furthermore, we conduct extensive experiments across several 3DMM bases and fitting algorithms, uncovering systematic age-related biases and providing preliminary evidence of biases associated with gender and ethnicity. Our findings highlight the necessity of adopting curvature-aware evaluation protocols to ensure demographic fairness and geometric precision in future 3D face reconstruction research.

</details>


### [61] [HIVE: Understanding Post-Hallucination Reasoning in Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07507)
*Feng He,Zhenting Wang,Qifan Wang,Qiang Guan,Dongfang Liu,Ruixiang Tang,Qiankun Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces HIVE, an evaluation framework for studying Post Hallucination Reasoning (PHR) in VLMs. It finds that hallucinated captions can improve accuracy on vision-language tasks but have limited/unstable effects on text-only tasks, revealing structured modality-dependent patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work focuses on detecting or suppressing hallucinations at generation time, leaving the subsequent reasoning stage (Post Hallucination Reasoning) largely unexplored. Understanding how hallucinated semantics influence downstream reasoning is critical for improving multimodal system reliability.

Method: The authors introduce HIVE (Hallucination Inference and Verification Engine), an evaluation infrastructure enabling controlled comparisons between faithful and hallucinated captions. They evaluate across nine tasks and nine models, analyzing modality-dependent patterns.

Result: Hallucinated captions often improve accuracy on vision-language tasks, while text-only tasks exhibit limited or unstable effects. Hallucinated cues broaden semantic coverage and reshape reasoning dynamics while preserving stable inference.

Conclusion: Hallucinated semantics may influence downstream reasoning once they enter the model's inference context. Understanding this post-hallucination stage is important for improving the reliability and interpretability of multimodal reasoning systems.

Abstract: Hallucinations in vision language models (VLMs) are commonly treated as semantic errors, yet they often arise from partial or ambiguous visual evidence. Prior work mainly focuses on detecting or suppressing hallucinations at generation time, leaving the subsequent reasoning stage largely unexplored. In this work, we study Post Hallucination Reasoning (PHR), the stage in which hallucinated semantics enter the model's inference context and influence downstream predictions. To systematically investigate PHR, we introduce HIVE, Hallucination Inference and Verification Engine, an evaluation infrastructure that enables controlled comparisons between faithful and hallucinated captions. Across nine tasks and nine models, we observe structured modality dependent patterns: hallucinated captions often improve accuracy on vision language tasks, while text only tasks exhibit limited or unstable effects. Further analyses show that hallucinated cues broaden semantic coverage and reshape reasoning dynamics while preserving stable inference. These findings highlight that hallucinated semantics may influence downstream reasoning once they enter the model's inference context. Understanding this post hallucination stage is important for improving the reliability and interpretability of multimodal reasoning systems. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/hefengcs/HIVE.

</details>


### [62] [Learning to Unify Deformable Shape and Texture Representations for Cardiac Video Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07518)
*Tonmoy Hossain,Miaomiao Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a cardiac video classification model that fuses deformable shape and image texture features via bi-directional cross-attention in latent space, with dynamic temporal weighting across cardiac phases, achieving state-of-the-art performance on CMR video data with improved interpretability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing deep networks for cardiac image classification use simple concatenation to combine deformable shape and texture features, which fails to exploit their complementary nature, learn cross-modal dependencies, or account for varying diagnostic importance across different cardiac phases.

Method: A bi-directional cross-attention mechanism in the latent space fuses deformable shape and image texture representations, enabling each modality to adaptively weight the other based on spatio-temporal correspondence, with dynamic temporal weighting that adjusts modality contributions across cardiac phases.

Result: State-of-the-art classification performance on a cine cardiac magnetic resonance (CMR) video dataset, with improved interpretability via attention mechanisms that identify diagnostically critical cardiac phases and modality contributions.

Conclusion: Learning temporal features in an integrated space of deformable shape and image texture via cross-attention with dynamic phase weighting outperforms simple concatenation-based fusion and uniform temporal attention, offering both accuracy and interpretability gains.

Abstract: Deformable shape representations have proven to be robust complements to texture features in cardiac image classification, offering geometric priors that are invariant to imaging artifacts and intensity variations. However, existing deep networks perform simple concatenation to combine these distinct feature representations, which neither fully exploits their complementary nature nor learns cross-modal feature dependencies. Furthermore, this results in uniform attention across all timepoints; hence ignoring the varying diagnostic importance across the cardiac phases. In this paper, we propose a novel cardiac video classification model that, for the first time, learns temporal features in an integrated space of deformable shape and image texture representations. In particular, we design a bi-directional cross-attention in the latent space to fuse latent deformable shape and image features, allowing each modality to adaptively weight the other based on spatio-temporal correspondence. In contrast to current methods that apply uniform weighting across all the cardiac phases, our approach learns to dynamically adjust the contributions of shape and texture representations, derived from images, over time. We demonstrate state-of-the-art classification performance on a cine cardiac magnetic resonance (CMR) video dataset, achieving improved interpretability from attention mechanisms that identify diagnostically critical cardiac phases and modality contributions.

</details>


### [63] [Context-Aware Slum Mapping in Sub-Saharan Africa Using Sentinel-1 Texture and Local Climate Zones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07532)
*Peterson Chepkilot,Babak Memar,Paolo Gamba*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A context-aware Optical-SAR framework integrating Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 data significantly improves informal settlement (LCZ 7) mapping in SSA cities, achieving 0.81 OA and reducing LCZ 3-7 confusion to 7%, with SAR textures providing dominant performance gains and stabilizing mapping across seasons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Optical imagery alone fails to distinguish informal settlements (LCZ 7) from spectrally similar formal compact low-rise areas (LCZ 3) in Sub-Saharan African cities, necessitating a more robust mapping approach.

Method: A three-tier SAR integration strategy (calibrated backscatter, GLCM textures, physics-guided feature) combined with Sentinel-2 spectral features within an adapted LCZ taxonomy, evaluated via stratified hold-out protocol and season-aware ablation study across Nairobi and Eldoret, Kenya.

Result: Optical-SAR model achieves OA of 0.816 (dry) and 0.807 (wet), outperforming WUDAPT baseline (0.704); reduces LCZ 3-7 confusion to 7%; SAR textures provide dominant performance gain and stabilize mapping across seasons; cross-city transfer remains limited without local adaptation.

Conclusion: Incorporating SAR-derived features yields consistent improvements for urban morphology mapping in data-scarce SSA environments across seasons and cities, though cross-city generalization requires local adaptation strategies.

Abstract: Accurate mapping of informal settlements remains a major challenge in Sub-Saharan African (SSA) cities because optical imagery often fails to distinguish Informal Settlements (defined here as LCZ 7) from spectrally similar formal Compact Low-Rise areas (LCZ 3). This study presents a context-aware, reproducible Optical-SAR framework that improves informal settlement delineation using Sentinel-2 spectral features and Sentinel-1 structural information within an adapted Local Climate Zone (LCZ) taxonomy. We implement a three-tier SAR integration strategy: calibrated backscatter, GLCM textures, and a physics-guided feature engineered to capture the high structural disorder and weak radar return characteristic of SSA informal settlements. Using reference data across Nairobi and Eldoret (Kenya), we evaluate performance via a stratified hold-out protocol and a season-aware ablation study. Results show that SAR textures provide the dominant performance gain for LCZ 7 detection. The Optical-SAR model achieves overall accuracy of 0.816 (dry) and 0.807 (wet), significantly outperforming the WUDAPT baseline (OA 0.704) and reducing the critical LCZ 3 - LCZ 7 confusion to 7%. Seasonal analysis indicates that while optical-only separability varies with phenology, SAR-derived textures stabilize informal settlement mapping across seasons. These findings demonstrate that the incorporation of SAR-derived features yields consistent improvements for urban morphology mapping in data-scarce environments across seasons and across the evaluated source cities, while cross-city transfer remains limited without local adaptation strategies.

</details>


### [64] [Infinite Worlds with Versatile Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07534)
*Zelin Gao,Qiuyu Wang,Jiapeng Zhu,Jingye Chen,Zichen Liu,Qingyan Bai,Jiahao Wang,Yufeng Yuan,Hanlin Wang,Yichong Lu,Ka Leong Cheng,Haojie Zhang,Jian Gao,Tianrui Feng,Yuzheng Liu,Yao Yao,Yinghao Xu,Xing Zhu,Yujun Shen,Hao Ouyang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LingBot-World 2.0 is an upgraded world model with infinite interaction horizon, real-time 60fps 720p video generation, diverse interactive elements, and a novel dual-agent architecture (pilot + director) for planning and environment synthesis, plus multi-player support and a lightweight 1.3B variant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance world modeling by achieving unbounded interaction horizons, real-time performance, higher diversity of interactive elements, and introducing agentic collaboration (pilot and director agents) for autonomous character control and dynamic environment synthesis.

Method: (1) Causal pretraining paradigm for unbounded horizon; (2) Distillation of a real-time variant from the base model for 60fps 720p video; (3) Expanded action set (attacking, archery, spell-casting, shooting) and text-driven events; (4) Novel dual-agent harness: a pilot agent plans/executes character behaviors, a director agent synthesizes new environmental elements; multi-player interface; 14B primary model + 1.3B lightweight variant.

Result: Unbounded interaction horizon with consistent quality; real-time 60fps 720p video; significantly more diverse interactive elements; successful integration of agentic planning and dynamic environment synthesis; multi-player shared experience; lightweight 1.3B model deployable on a single GPU.

Conclusion: LingBot-World 2.0 achieves a more scalable, real-time, and interactive world simulator with autonomous agent collaboration, enabling richer and more persistent virtual experiences.

Abstract: We present LingBot-World 2.0 (also known as LingBot-World-Infinity), an advanced iteration of LingBot-World featuring four distinct upgrades. (1) Our model achieves an unbounded interaction horizon while maintaining consistent output quality, benefiting from a carefully crafted causal pretraining paradigm. (2) Through distilling a real-time variant from the base model, our system guarantees rapid response time, sufficient to drive 720p video streams at 60 fps. (3) Compared to the previous version, this update introduces highly diverse interactive elements, comprising a broader spectrum of actions (e.g., attacking, archery, spell-casting, and shooting) alongside a richer variety of text-driven events. (4) We pioneer the integration of an agentic harness within the domain of world modeling, wherein a pilot agent is tasked with planning and executing character behaviors, while a director agent is responsible for synthesizing novel environmental elements as the scene progresses. Additionally, to facilitate a shared experience, we develop an interface that permits multiple players to simultaneously immerse themselves in this vivid world simulator. We pair our primary 14B model with a lightweight 1.3B counterpart, which supports effortless deployment on a single GPU.

</details>


### [65] [Face-trace: Open-Set Attribution and Progressive Discovery of Synthetic Face Generators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07545)
*Alessia Infantino,Claudio Schiavella,Irene Amerini*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a pipeline for open-set synthetic face source attribution that combines known generator classification using frozen I-JEPA embeddings, energy-based OOD rejection, and unknown generator discovery via clustering of projected I-JEPA features and Forensic Self-Descriptors, achieving strong results on the WILD dataset in closed-set, open-set, and incremental settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing synthetic face attribution methods operate in a closed-set setting, assuming all possible generators are known during training, which does not reflect real-world scenarios where new generators continuously emerge and rejected samples need to be organized rather than discarded.

Method: A three-stage pipeline: (1) known generator classification using a classifier trained on frozen I-JEPA embeddings, (2) energy-based OOD rejection for detecting samples from unseen generators, (3) unknown generator discovery by combining projected I-JEPA features with Forensic Self-Descriptors followed by clustering, extended to an incremental scenario where rejected samples arrive over time.

Result: 96.73% closed-set attribution accuracy; 71.25% balanced accuracy for open-set rejection; clustering of unknown generators achieved ARI of 0.81, NMI of 0.90, 87.74% purity; incremental setting reached 99.23% final purity; cross-dataset experiments show generalization beyond original distribution.

Conclusion: The proposed pipeline effectively handles open-set synthetic face attribution, enabling both known generator classification and meaningful grouping of unknown generators, with promising cross-dataset generalization though post-processing remains a challenge.

Abstract: Recent advances in generative Artificial Intelligence have made synthetic face images increasingly realistic, creating new challenges for multimedia forensics. Source attribution methods should not only identify the generator of an image when the source is known, but also handle samples produced by previously unseen models. However, most existing approaches address synthetic face attribution in a closed-set setting, where all possible generators are available during training. This assumption does not hold in real-world scenarios, where new generators continuously appear and rejected samples should be organized rather than simply discarded. In this work we propose a pipeline for open-set synthetic face source attribution that combines known generator classification, energy-based OOD rejection, and unknown generator discovery. A classifier is trained on known generators using frozen I-JEPA embeddings, while rejected samples are represented by combining projected I-JEPA features with Forensic Self-Descriptors and then clustered to discover groups of unknown generators. We also extend the discovery stage to an incremental scenario, where rejected samples arrive over time. Experiments on the WILD dataset show that the proposed method achieves 96.73% closed-set attribution accuracy. In the open-set setting, energy-based rejection reaches 71.25% balanced accuracy, while rejected samples are clustered into meaningful unknown-generator groups, obtaining an ARI of 0.81, an NMI of 0.90, and an overall clustering purity of 87.74%. In the incremental setting, the discovered generator space is progressively extended while maintaining a final purity of 99.23%. Cross-dataset experiments suggest that the pipeline can operate beyond the original dataset distribution, although post-processing remains challenging.

</details>


### [66] [AA-ViT: Anatomically Aware Vision Transformer with Structural and Frequency Guidance for Contrast Enhanced Brain MRI Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07553)
*Talha Meraj,Tom Flannery,Charlie Cummins,Matt Townend,Thomas C Booth,Peter Crossley,Michael McCann,Ian Overton,Saritha Unnikrishnan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AA-ViT is a vision transformer for synthesizing contrast-enhanced MRI from non-contrast scans, using frequency-and-structure guidance to preserve anatomical boundaries. It achieves state-of-the-art PSNR/SSIM on BraTS 2021 and scores 3.94/5 in clinical evaluation by neuroradiologists and a neurosurgeon, demonstrating potential to avoid gadolinium contrast agents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard MRI often requires contrast agents (gadolinium) for tumour localization, but these agents are not always feasible (e.g., contraindicated in renal impairment). Developing non-invasive contrast-enhanced MRI synthesis methods is clinically important to avoid risks and expand patient access.

Method: The authors propose AA-ViT (Anatomically Aware Frequency-and-Structure-Guided Vision Transformer), which leverages frequency-domain and structural guidance to synthesize CEMRI from pre-contrast T1, T2, and FLAIR modalities, explicitly preserving anatomical boundaries and tumour-specific fine structures.

Result: On BraTS 2021, AA-ViT achieves higher PSNR and SSIM than existing state-of-the-art methods. Clinical evaluation on 19 glioma cases by three neuroradiologists and a neurosurgeon yields a mean score of 3.94/5, offering preliminary clinical validation.

Conclusion: The proposed AA-ViT can effectively synthesize contrast-enhanced MRI from non-contrast scans, potentially reducing scanning costs, shortening imaging time, and avoiding risks associated with gadolinium-based contrast agents.

Abstract: Accurate tumour localization and diagnosis is a critical component of clinical care for brain cancers. Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) is the most commonly used imaging modality due to its superior soft-tissue contrast. However, standard MRI often exhibits limited contrast and imaging artifacts, which necessitates the use of contrast agents to enhance lesion visibility. The administration of chemical contrast agents is not always feasible and may be contraindicated in patients with renal impairment or other health conditions. As a result, developing accurate and non-invasive contrast enhanced MRI (CEMRI) synthesis methods has clinical importance. In recent years, numerous approaches for CEMRI synthesis have been proposed, predominantly relying on generative artificial intelligence models. While these methods demonstrate promising performance, their dependence on implicit feature learning often limits their ability to preserve anatomical boundaries and tumour-specific fine structures. To address these challenges, we propose an anatomically aware frequency-and-structure-guided vision transformer (AA-ViT), for CEMRI synthesis using pre-contrast MRI modalities (T1, T2, and FLAIR). Experiments on the BraTS 2021 dataset demonstrate that the proposed method preserves anatomical and lesion boundaries, achieving higher PSNR and SSIM than state-of-the-art approaches. Clinical evaluation by three neuroradiologists and a neurosurgeon on 19 randomly selected cases across diverse gliomas yielded a mean score of 3.94/5, providing preliminary clinical validation rarely seen in prior studies. Synthetic post-contrast scans from our model could lower scanning costs, shorten imaging time, and avoid the potential risks of using gadolinium-based contrast agents.

</details>


### [67] [Automatic Echocardiography Segmentation via Transition Probability Correlation for Stable Semantic Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07580)
*Xinran Chen,Xiyuan Wang,Guangquan Zhou,Chuan Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A convolution-based network with a novel STLSF module (combining window-matching semantic correction and semantics-guided texture enhancement) and frequency-aware denoising pre-training achieves state-of-the-art echocardiography segmentation (93.87% Dice on CAMUS, 92.62% on EchoNet-Dynamic).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Echocardiography suffers from speckle noise, low SNR, ambiguous semantic features, and fragmented boundaries, which degrade deep learning segmentation accuracy; temporal motion cues are also underutilized.

Method: Proposes (1) STLSF module with window-matching-based semantic correction (using local transition probability correlations) and semantics-guided texture enhancement; (2) frequency-aware denoising pre-training to learn ultrasound-specific imaging priors; built on a convolution-based network with locality inductive bias and long-range dependencies.

Result: 93.87% Dice (3.29mm HD95) on CAMUS; 92.62% Dice (2.73mm HD95) on EchoNet-Dynamic; SOTA performance.

Conclusion: The proposed methods effectively mitigate texture instability and ambiguous semantics from poor echocardiography quality, achieving superior segmentation accuracy.

Abstract: While echocardiography is essential for cardiovascular diagnosis, inherent speckle noise and low signal-to-noise ratio often lead to ambiguous semantic features and fragmented boundaries. These limitations significantly hinder the segmentation accuracy of deep learning models in complex clinical cases. Moreover, temporal motion of the heart plays a critical role in recognizing anatomical structures. To address these challenges, we designed a STLSF module which comprises a window-matching-based semantic correction component and a semantics-guided texture enhancement component. By leveraging local transition probability correlations to correct semantics and employing semantics-guided texture enhancement, the STLSF module effectively mitigates texture instability and ambiguous semantic interpretations caused by disadvantaged echocardiography quality. Additionally, to facilitate the encoder's adaptation to the intrinsic priors of ultrasound-specific imaging patterns, we propose a frequency-aware denoising pre-training method. The entire work builds a convolution-based network with locality inductive bias and long-range dependencies. Extensive experiments confirm our SOTA performance, achieving 93.87\% Dice on CAMUS and 92.62\% on EchoNet-Dynamic, with respective HD95 values of 3.29mm and 2.73mm.

</details>


### [68] [Cardiac MRI Through-Plane Super-Resolution Guided by Reference and Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07581)
*Shaoming Pan,Chenchuhui Hu,Leon Axel,Meng Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: STRMSR is a reference- and memory-guided through-plane super-resolution framework for cardiac MRI that uses HR reference views and intermediate SR results to reconstruct high-resolution 3D volumes, achieving consistent improvements over baselines at 4x and 8x upsampling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Clinical cardiac MRI suffers from coarse through-plane resolution due to scan time and motion constraints, limiting 3D analysis and diagnostic accuracy.

Method: STRMSR leverages HR reference views from the same subject and intermediate SR results as memory, using coarse-to-fine contextual matching for robust correspondence and a learnable patch-wise dynamic feature aggregation module for content-adaptive fusion.

Result: Consistent improvements over baselines on the WHS cardiac MRI dataset under orthogonal-plane and long-axis chamber view protocols at 4x and 8x upsampling factors.

Conclusion: The proposed STRMSR framework effectively enhances through-plane resolution in cardiac MRI, improving 3D volume consistency and diagnostic potential.

Abstract: Clinical cardiac MRI is commonly acquired with high in-plane resolution but coarse through-plane resolution to reduce scan time and accommodate breath-hold and cardiac-motion constraints, which limits 3D analysis and diagnostic accuracy. We propose STRMSR, a reference- and memory-guided through-plane super-resolution (SR) framework that reconstructs high-resolution (HR) cardiac volumes by leveraging HR reference views acquired from the same subject and intermediate SR results as the memory. Our method uses coarse-to-fine contextual matching to establish robust correspondence between low-resolution target and reference/memory images under spatial misalignment. A learnable patch-wise dynamic feature aggregation module predicts content-adaptive mixture weights for each local patch, effectively fusing dynamic information while suppressing unreliable feature transfers. The intermediate SR results stored in the memory bank ensure slice-to-slice consistency for the super-resolved 3D volume. Experiments on the WHS cardiac MRI dataset under two reference protocols, orthogonal-plane views and long-axis chamber views, demonstrate consistent improvements over baselines at 4x and 8x upsampling factors.

</details>


### [69] [MedPMC: A Systematic Framework for Scaling High-Fidelity Medical Multimodal Data for Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07673)
*Hyunjae Kim,Dain Kim,Pan Xiao,Serina S. Applebaum,Younjoon Chung,Xuguang Ai,Yu Yin,Roy Jiang,Yuexi Du,Yawen Wei,Yiming Kong,Tuo Guo,Zhiyuan Cao,Mengmeng Du,Yuelei Fu,Yan Hu,Rui Shi,Gui Yang,Kevin W. Jin,Yuntian Liu,Yuxuan Tian,Jonathan Marquez,Zhen Chen,Sheng Zhang,Hoifung Poon,Hua Xu,Jaewoo Kang,Qingyu Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MedPMC is an automated framework that transforms 6.1 million PMC articles into 11 million high-fidelity medical image-text pairs, significantly outperforming prior datasets and models across 26 benchmarks and a clinical dermatology deployment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Limited access to large-scale, high-quality clinical data hinders the development of medical multimodal foundation models. Existing PMC-derived resources suffer from poor fidelity, reproducibility, and lack clinical validation.

Method: MedPMC is an automated, continuously updatable framework applied to 6.1 million PMC articles, curating 11 million image-text pairs with components for screening (F1=93.2), multi-panel detection (F1=96.5), figure separation (mAP=89.8), caption alignment (F1=81.4; ROUGE-L=85.3), and medical figure classification (F1=96.5).

Result: Manual review found 95.3% of MedPMC images medically relevant vs 19.7% in prior dataset. MedPMC-trained CLIP improved zero-shot AUC by 7.1 pp over strongest baseline using < half the image-text pairs. Improved medical VQA by 1.9 and 16.9 pp, and dermatology morphology-to-image retrieval Recall@5 by 11.7 pp in Yale clinical deployment.

Conclusion: High-fidelity literature curation strengthens medical multimodal foundation models across both benchmark and clinical settings. The framework, corpus, benchmarks, and pretrained models are publicly released.

Abstract: Medicine is inherently multimodal, requiring clinicians to synthesize information across diverse data streams. Yet the development of multimodal foundation models is constrained by limited access to large-scale, high-quality clinical data. Although PubMed Central (PMC) offers a complementary source of expert-authored image-text data, existing PMC-derived resources remain limited in fidelity, reproducibility, and clinical validation. We introduce MedPMC, an automated, continuously updatable framework that transforms permissively licensed literature into high-fidelity infrastructure for medical multimodal models. Applied to 6.1 million PMC articles, MedPMC curated 11 million medical image-text pairs. Component evaluations showed strong performance for initial screening (F1 = 93.2), multi-panel figure detection (F1 = 96.5), figure separation (mAP = 89.8), caption separation and alignment (F1 = 81.4; ROUGE-L = 85.3), and medical figure classification (F1 = 96.5). Manual review by five annotators, three with medical training, found 95.3% of MedPMC images medically relevant, versus 19.7% in a prior PMC-derived dataset. Across 26 benchmarks spanning 11 specialties, a MedPMC-trained CLIP-style model improved average zero-shot AUC by 7.1 percentage points over the strongest architecture-matched biomedical CLIP baseline despite using fewer than half as many image-text pairs. As the vision encoder in a multimodal large language model, it improved medical visual question-answering by 1.9 and 16.9 percentage points across two benchmarks. In 10,524 Yale New Haven Health System dermatology photographs, it improved morphology-to-image retrieval Recall@5 by 11.7 percentage points. These findings show that high-fidelity literature curation strengthens medical multimodal foundation models across benchmark and clinical settings. We publicly release the framework, corpus, benchmarks, and pretrained models.

</details>


### [70] [Scaling Mixture-of-Experts Video Pretraining for Embodied Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07675)
*Shuailei Ma,Jiaqi Liao,Xinyang Wang,Jingjing Wang,Chaoran Feng,Zijing Hu,Chong Bao,Zichen Xi,Yuqi Gan,Weisen Wang,Yanhong Zeng,Qin Zhao,Zifan Shi,Wei Wu,Hao Ouyang,Qiuyu Wang,Shangzhan Zhang,Jiahao Shao,Yipengjing Sun,Liangxiao Hu,Lunke Pan,Nan Xue,Kecheng Zheng,Yinghao Xu,Xing Zhu,Yujun Shen,Ka Leong Cheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LingBot-Video is a DiT-based video pretraining paradigm using Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture, robot-oriented data profiling, and a multi-dimensional reward system to bridge the gap between video generation and embodied intelligence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Video generative models suffer from a domain mismatch for robot control because they prioritize visual fidelity and creativity over computational efficiency and physical realism, making them unsuitable for embodied intelligence tasks.

Method: 1) Architecture: Adopts Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) instead of dense framework for better capacity-efficiency trade-off. 2) Data: Constructs a data profiling engine augmenting internet videos with robot-oriented footage (manipulation, navigation, egocentric). 3) Training: Develops a multi-dimensional reward system enforcing physical rationality and task completion beyond standard criteria.

Result: Comprehensive evaluations validate its performance and efficiency as a video foundation model for embodied intelligence.

Conclusion: LingBot-Video is the inaugural large-scale, open-source MoE video foundation model, pioneering the bridge between digital creativity and physical actuation.

Abstract: Despite the recent promise in robot control, video generative models suffer from a domain mismatch due to their primary focus on content creation. For example, their design inherently prioritizes visual fidelity and creativity over computational efficiency and physical realism. In this work, we present LingBot-Video, a DiT-based video pretraining paradigm specifically tailored for embodied intelligence. From the architecture perspective, we adopt the Mixture-of-Experts (MoE), instead of dense, framework to achieve a better trade-off between modeling capacity and inference efficiency, and manage to scale it up from scratch. From the data perspective, we construct a data profiling engine that augments standard internet videos with extensive robot-oriented footage, encompassing manipulation, navigation, and egocentric perspectives, to equip the base model with an intrinsic understanding of actions and world dynamics. From the training perspective, we develop a multi-dimensional reward system to enforce the alignment regarding physical rationality and task completion, going beyond standard criteria such as aesthetics, prompt-following, and motion consistency. Comprehensive evaluations validate its performance and efficiency as a video foundation model. We contribute LingBot-Video as the inaugural large-scale, open-source MoE video foundation model to the community, in a pioneering effort to bridge digital creativity and physical actuation.

</details>


<div id='cs.LG'></div>

# cs.LG [[Back]](#toc)

### [71] [TriRoute: Unified Learned Routing for Joint Adaptive Attention, Experts, and KV-Cache Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06601)
*Andrii Balashov,Olena Ponomarova*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TriRoute introduces a lightweight controller that jointly decides attention mode, expert selection, and KV-cache bit-width per token per layer, achieving Pareto-dominant efficiency over independent MoD+MoE+KV-quantization combinations while preserving robustness on rare entities and code.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing conditional computation techniques (MoE, MoD, KV-cache quantization) are applied independently on single axes, but these decisions are strongly coupled—e.g., a token needing full attention may also need high-precision caching—so joint coordination can yield better quality-efficiency trade-offs.

Method: TriRoute uses a single shared lightweight controller that emits a coordinated policy per token per layer: (i) attention mode (skip/local/full), (ii) sparse FFN experts (with a null expert), and (iii) KV-cache bit-width. Training uses heterogeneous relaxation (Gumbel-Softmax with straight-through estimation, load-balanced top-k gating) under a Lagrangian budget constraint. Addresses cross-axis routing-collapse cascade via per-axis normalization and coupling-aware balancing loss.

Result: On decoder-only models from 160M to 1.3B parameters at compute-optimal token counts, TriRoute Pareto-dominates the best independent MoD+MoE+KV-quantization combination at matched inference FLOPs and memory, better preserving tail-case robustness on rare entities, code, and arithmetic.

Conclusion: Jointly routing across attention, expert, and cache dimensions yields better efficiency-quality trade-offs than independent methods. The learned policies are interpretable: full attention and high-precision cache are allocated to sentence-initial positions, rare subwords, and named entities, while function words are routed cheaply.

Abstract: Conditional computation can decouple language model quality from per-token inference cost, yet leading techniques act on a single axis in isolation: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) sparsifies the FFN, Mixture-of-Depths (MoD) skips whole transformer blocks, and KV-cache quantization compresses attention memory. We argue these three decisions (attention resolution, expert selection, and cache bit-width) are strongly coupled and should be made jointly: a token rare enough to warrant full attention may also need high-precision caching regardless of which expert processes it. We introduce TriRoute, a single lightweight controller shared across all three axes that, for every token at every layer, emits a coordinated policy: (i) an attention mode (skip/local/full), (ii) a sparse set of FFN experts (with a null expert recovering MoD), and (iii) a KV-cache bit-width. The controller trains end-to-end via a heterogeneous relaxation (Gumbel-Softmax with straight-through estimation for categorical decisions and load-balanced top-k gating for experts) under a Lagrangian budget constraint that turns the average compute and memory cost into a controllable knob. We identify a cross-axis routing-collapse cascade in naive joint training, where collapse on one axis propagates to the others, and address it with per-axis normalization and a coupling-aware balancing loss. On decoder-only models from 160M to 1.3B parameters at compute-optimal token counts, TriRoute Pareto-dominates the best independent MoD+MoE+KV-quantization combination at matched inference FLOPs and memory, while better preserving tail-case robustness on rare entities, code, and arithmetic that pure perplexity optimization erodes. Post-hoc analysis reveals interpretable structure: the controller allocates full attention and high-precision cache to sentence-initial positions, rare subwords, and named entities, while cheaply routing function words.

</details>


### [72] [A Quiet Failure in Calibrated Virtual Screening: Marginal Conformal Prediction Under-Covers the Minority Class, and a Class-Conditional Fix Recovers It](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06605)
*Muhammadjon Tursunbadalov,Mustafojon Tursunbadalov*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Conformal prediction's coverage guarantee fails on imbalanced drug-discovery datasets, severely undercovering minority classes (e.g., 4.2% on toxicity) while maintaining high global coverage—a hidden failure that class-conditional conformal prediction fixes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conformal prediction is widely adopted in drug discovery to provide reliable prediction sets, but its standard (marginal) guarantee may fail on imbalanced datasets. The authors aim to demonstrate and diagnose this hidden failure in real chemistry.

Method: The authors test marginal conformal prediction on four imbalanced drug-discovery datasets (e.g., blood-brain barrier, clinical-trial toxicity) with three model types (random forest, graph network, frozen chemical language model). They derive a conservation identity linking minority shortfall to imbalance ratio, and apply class-conditional (Mondrian) conformal prediction as a fix.

Result: Marginal conformal prediction achieves 90% global coverage but minority coverage falls to 64.8% (blood-brain barrier) and 4.2% (toxicity). The conservation identity predicts the gap within one point. The failure persists across scaffold splits and a second conformal score. Mondrian conformal prediction restores minority coverage to target with modest prediction-set size increase.

Conclusion: Standard conformal prediction's marginal guarantee is unreliable under class imbalance—a dangerous and invisible failure. Class-conditional conformal prediction provides a practical fix. The authors also propose a diagnostic and show that abstaining on affected compounds flips screening campaign utility from negative to positive.

Abstract: Conformal prediction is being adopted in drug discovery to put an honest number on model reliability: pick an error rate alpha, and the method returns prediction sets containing the true label with probability at least 1 - alpha. We show this guarantee can be dangerous on imbalanced datasets. Across four datasets, standard (marginal) conformal prediction hits its global 90% coverage target while leaving the minority class badly exposed: realized minority coverage falls to 64.8% on blood-brain-barrier penetration and to 4.2% on clinical-trial toxicity, where the rare class is nearly abandoned. The failure is not tied to one model: a random forest, a graph network, and a frozen chemical language model all reproduce it (p < 0.001 in every case), with severity tracking baseline calibration on rare labels rather than architecture. A conservation identity explains the effect: the minority's shortfall equals the majority's surplus amplified by the imbalance ratio, predicting the measured gap to within one point and ordering severity across datasets. The failure survives realistic scaffold splits and a second conformal score, while aggregate accuracy and overall coverage stay reassuringly high, which is exactly why it is easy to miss. Class-conditional (Mondrian) conformal prediction closes the gap on every dataset, restoring minority coverage to target for a modest increase in prediction-set size. We localize the failures to generic molecular scaffolds - plain benzene and pyridine cores occurring in both classes - propose a one-number diagnostic, and show with a cost model that abstaining on affected compounds flips a screening campaign from net-negative to net-positive utility. Our contribution is demonstrating on real chemistry how severe and invisible this known conformal-theory gap becomes under imbalance, and laying out a practical protocol restoring per-class reliability.

</details>


### [73] [NEST: Tackling Dataset-Level Distribution Shifts via Regime-Oriented Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06607)
*Lanhao Li,Bingshu Xie,Lijun Sun,Xin Xue,Haoyi Zhou,Jianxin Li*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: NEST is a two-phase dense Mixture-of-Experts framework that models dataset-level distribution shifts in multivariate time-series by partitioning data into distinct operational regimes via moment-entropy clustering, using a regime-oriented router with geometric modulation, and employing regime-specific expert kernels with variate-attention patterns, achieving SOTA on heterogeneous benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing forecasting methods focus on local temporal shifts but fail to address the global structural challenge where datasets are composites of distinct operational regimes, leading to poor long-term forecasting under dataset-level distribution shifts.

Method: NEST uses a two-phase dense MoE architecture: (1) unsupervised clustering in moment-entropy space to partition the dataset into distinct regimes; (2) a regime-oriented router generating initial expert weights from temporal content, refined by geometric modulation to regime centroids; (3) individual experts as specialized kernels capturing regime-specific dynamics via unique variate-attention patterns.

Result: Consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance on diverse benchmarks including heterogeneous network traffic and physical phenomena.

Conclusion: Explicitly modeling and recomposing evolving structural regimes via dense MoE with regime-oriented routing and specialized expert kernels significantly improves long-term forecasting under distribution shifts.

Abstract: Accurate long-term forecasting in complex systems is frequently compromised by dataset-level distribution shifts, where diverse underlying behavioral modes and evolving system states drive the dynamic multivariate time-series. While existing methods predominantly focus on local temporal shifts, they fail to explicitly model the global structural challenge where datasets are composites of distinct operational regimes. In this paper, we propose NEST, a specialized framework designed to model and recompose these evolving structures through a two-phase dense MoE architecture. NEST first facilitates structural specialization by partitioning the dataset into distinct operational regimes through unsupervised clustering in a principled moment-entropy space. We introduce a regime-oriented router mechanism that generates initial expert weights based on temporal content, subsequently refined through geometric modulation to regime centroids. Crucially, rather than acting as monolithic predictors, individual experts function as specialized kernels that capture regime-specific dynamics by evolving unique variate-attention patterns. Extensive evaluations on diverse benchmarks, including heterogeneous network traffic and physical phenomena, demonstrate that NEST consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance. Our code and datasets are available at https://github.com/Aaralshin/NEST

</details>


### [74] [D2PO: Optimizing Diffusion Samplers via Dynamic Preference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06609)
*Jinkyu Kim,Jinyoung Choi,Bohyung Han*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: D2PO reformulates diffusion sampler optimization as a preference-based alignment problem using DPO, modeling sampling policy as an EBM and introducing dynamic preferences for self-improving refinement, outperforming regression-based schedulers under low-NFE constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
